# Predictions ledger

> **Private research — not for the public site.** Every forward-looking claim found in the 24 X Space transcripts under `catalog/spaces/transcripts/`, graded against what happened as of **2026-10-31**. Machine-readable copy: [`predictions-ledger.json`](predictions-ledger.json).

## Summary

- **306 predictions** logged: 294 forward predictions, plus 12 retro claims ("we called it" claims about older forecasts). Retro claims are graded but kept out of the main stats.
- Of the 294 forward predictions, **52 could be decided** (hit, partial or miss): **2 hits, 17 partials, 33 misses**. 152 were unfalsifiable, 76 are still pending and 14 are unresolvable.
- Most decided calls were short, dated forecasts for 2025–2026: Iran or nuclear escalation, big events in specific April 2026 windows, blackouts, lockdowns, fuel prices and shortages. Most missed. Wars did happen and energy did get worse, but the specific dated calls usually did not land.
- \* *Hit rate* = hits ÷ decided (hit + partial + miss). *wtd* counts a partial as half a hit.

## Method

**Source.** I read every line of all 24 `.txt`/`.vtt` transcript pairs. Timestamps come from the matching `.vtt` cue, which follows the edited, published audio (pre-show cuts are already applied). Agents read the transcripts in overlapping chunks, and overlaps were removed. The "date said" is the `date:` in the episode's `catalog/posts/<slug>-space.md` stub. Speaker is inferred from context, because the transcripts have no speaker labels, and each speaker carries a confidence level in the JSON.

**What counted as a prediction.** A claim, made on the recording, that something public and observable would (or would not) happen after the recording date. This includes astrological forecasts of world events, remote-viewing results presented as future events, forecasts about named people, geopolitical/economic/AI/disclosure forecasts, and personal claims that can be checked publicly. Vague forward claims were logged too, so they could be labelled unfalsifiable instead of left out. Excluded: worldview or metaphysics, abstract readings of a chart with no forward event, hypotheticals, jokes, and other people's forecasts the speaker reported without endorsing. A forecast repeated within an episode is logged once, at its earliest timestamp; the other timestamps go in `also_at`. Retro claims are kept separately.

**Grades.**

- **hit**: the specific claim happened, inside the stated or clearly implied window. A loosely related event, or something already under way when the claim was made, is not a hit.
- **partial**: a material part came true, but the magnitude, place or timing was off, or only part of a multi-part claim happened. Also used for a specific claim with no window that has since come true, and capped there for claims about an unnamed member of a large group with an inferred window.
- **miss**: the window passed and the event did not happen, or the opposite happened. Hedged wording ("could", "might") is graded on the same scale and flagged `hedged`. A hedge does not turn a miss into "unresolvable".
- **unresolvable**: checkable in principle, but reliable public sources do not settle it. This also covers conditional claims whose condition never occurred, and retro claims whose original forecast cannot be found in a public record.
- **still pending**: the window is still open (or none was given), and the claim has neither come true nor been ruled out.
- **unfalsifiable**: no observable test could separate true from false ("things will get crazier", "a spiritual awakening", "a very visible event" in a week of active war), or the claim is so broad that the base rate makes it near-certain.

Outcomes were checked with web searches, preferring wire services, major papers and official data (EIA, AAA, NOAA/SWPC, IDC, NASA, WHO). Every hit, partial and miss cites at least one source. Windows inferred from relative wording ("next month", "this summer") are marked *(inferred)*. Graders checked them, and several were tightened. Sub-agents graded in parallel. A coordinator pass then made calls on shared events consistent, re-checked every hit, and changed two grades (P101 from hit to partial, P141 from miss to partial). Changed rows are flagged `coordinator_override` in the JSON.

**Limitations.**

- **Transcription errors.** Whisper output is unedited. Names and terms are often misheard (e.g. "Saturn Neption"), and long dead-air stretches render as repeated "Thank you." Quotes are verbatim *transcript* text, not verified against the audio. Before quoting one publicly, check it against the audio.
- **Speaker attribution.** Speakers are inferred, not labelled. Guest and caller lines may be attributed to the wrong person, so see `speaker_confidence`.
- **Coverage.** Only Spaces with committed transcripts are included. `2025-06-20-sacred-time-and-synchronicity-storytime` holds only about 8 minutes of pre-show and has no predictions. JT's posts, other Spaces and later Peak Mind episodes not yet in the repo are not covered. Extraction is judgement-based, so a reader could reasonably add or drop borderline items, especially among the vague ones.
- **Grading is as of 2026-10-31.** It relies on news coverage that may later be revised. The absence of an event (no blackout, no attack) is inferred from searches turning up nothing, which is strong for large events and weaker for small ones. Pending items should be re-graded when their windows close (e.g. Nov 3, 2026 election-day calls, winter 2026–27, 2027 items).
- **Dates.** The stub dates follow the slugs. For scheduled Spaces, yt-dlp's `upload_date` can differ from the day the Space went live by a day or more (see PR #7), so "date said" may be off by about a day for some episodes.
- **Astrology windows.** These use the tropical ephemeris dates JT himself used (e.g. Mars to 0° Aries on 2026-04-09). Graders checked them where the claim depended on them.

## Ledger by episode

Columns: **ID** · **Time**: timestamp in the edited audio (`.vtt`) · **Speaker** · **Quote**: verbatim transcript · **Claim** · **Basis** · **Target**: as stated, then the parsed window · **Grade** (🔸 = hedged, ⓡ = retro claim) · **Outcome**, with sources.
